ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u27	I understand that you want a written answer to all your questions. [[Yes, sir.]] In that case, I ask for an additional question if Mr Paweł Olszewski has it. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u28	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Today, I have the honour of speaking for the first time after 16 years as a civil servant before the High Chamber as Head of Civil Service to answer the questions of the Members under the authority of the President of the Council of Ministers. Indeed, I will answer them orally to the extent that I have the data prepared and, to the rest of the extent that you are asking, this reply will be given in writing. On the date of entry into force of the amendment of the Civil Service Act, the last amendment, in higher positions in the civil service of the Prime Minister's office, in ministries, central offices, voivodships and voivodships of veterinary inspectors, because in these groups of offices there were higher posts within the meaning of the Act, a total of 1580 people were employed. As a result of the application of Article. 6 the amendment, which actually provided for the termination of employment relationships of those employed in higher positions after 30 days, unless new employment conditions are proposed to them, has expired on the basis of this provision a total of 212 persons employed in higher posts, i.e. around 13% the total number of higher posts. In each group of offices, these figures are as follows: in ministries of 780 employed persons, the employment relationship expired by 105 persons, i.e. 13%. , in central offices of 432 persons employment relationships expired 29, i.e. 7% , and in the voivodship offices and veterinary inspectors of almost 370 people employed in higher positions, the employment relationship expired to 78 people, i.e. around 21%. In this respect, which Members ask, i.e. how many of these people were in a protective period and in various other personal situations, we will try to gather information and give it in writing. However, I can already say today that from outside the civil service corps in higher positions in the civil service - those 1580 which existed at the time of the entry into force of the law, today these posts are slightly less - a total of 131 people were employed. This is not equivalent, High Izbo, except that these 131 people are people who do not have any experience at all in the civil service, because among them may have also found people who once, in the past, were already employed in government offices, while at the time of their appointment their professional status was such that they were outside the corps. And that's the number of 131 people. As far as party membership is concerned, the situation here is somewhat more complicated, because the Civil Service Act, both now and before the amendment, formulated a condition, i.e. prohibition of membership of a political party for persons employed in a higher position but during the period of employment. There was and is not a condition for the prohibition of membership of a political party within a period of 5 years or any period prior to taking a higher position in the civil service. Therefore, data on activity, membership of political parties before employment in the civil service are not collected and cannot in principle be collected. The labour law allows the employer to request a specific directory of information from the employee about it, a precise directory of data. For different personal data, including the course of a previous career, information may also be collected which is required to be provided under separate rules. Such a separate provision in our case would be the Civil Service Act, which, however, allows the candidate to request a statement from him that he is not and will not be a member of a political party, but does not allow him to request information or a statement as to whether he was a member of a political party in the past. Hence the lack of such data. Thank you very much.

Undersecretary of State of the Ministry of Justice Łukasz Piebiak: In answer to the Member's question and thanking him for this question - indeed the problem is the Ministry of Justice is aware of its existence. As you have rightly said, on a legal basis, the matter seems quite obvious, because it was at the request of the Minister of Justice... that is, the Minister of Justice challenged the resolution of the National Chambers Council, precisely the one according to which VAT had to be charged an additional enforcement fee, which, of course, increases the burden on the debtor, and the Supreme Court divided the rights of the Minister of Justice. This is a resolution. The Supreme Court, in Case III ZS 10/15 of the Supreme Court, repealing the resolution of the National Chambers Council of 15 September 2015. As you rightly point out, the position of the Ministry of Finance is clear in this respect and the bailiffs should not add VAT to the enforcement fee and this VAT is already included in the enforcement fee. In short, the burden on debtors should be lower and not higher. Unfortunately, the bailiffs do not want to comply with the quite clear rules that apply to them, claiming that they can give their own interpretation in their decisions to determine the amount of the enforcement fee and the Minister of Justice or the Minister of Finance, or that anyone else is not able to order anything here because they have a way of appeal to the regional courts in the context of a complaint against the actions of the bailiff. Regional courts, as far as we know, when recognising the actions of the bailiff, seem to share most of the view that there is no basis for adding VAT to the enforcement fee, but we also know the opposing judgments issued by the courts when recognising the actions of the bailiff. Of course, one might wonder whether, in such a situation, there is no basis for disciplinary action against bailiffs who do so. But this is a delicate matter, because it is a matter of their judicial or quasi-decision independence in determining the costs of execution proceedings. For this reason, it seems most appropriate to regulate this situation precisely, so that there is no doubt on the statutory road. And this issue is already in the area of interest appointed by the Minister of Justice of the Group to develop a draft law on bailiffs and a draft law on enforcement costs, in order to remove any doubts in this regard existing. At the moment, it is not the Minister of Justice, and the courts recognize the actions of the bailiff. I assume that, regardless of any change, the number of situations in which the courts will rule contrary to the Supreme Court, the interpretation of the Minister of Finance, the interpretation of the Tax Chamber, etc., will be negligible, but the case law of the courts of the Minister of Justice obviously has no influence. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u98	Thank you very much. Please respond to Minister Jack Bogucki. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u99	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In answer to Mr Robert Telus' question, I would like to inform you that this situation has been in place for a long time for producer groups since already on 15 and 19 November 2010 the European Commission conducted an investigation into the preliminary recognition and recognition plans of the groups in Poland. As a result of this investigation, the Commission considered that the checks carried out in respect of compliance by producer organisations with the recognition criteria were ineffective and not carried out at the frequency required by the rules, as well as before granting initial recognition to producer groups or recognition to producer organisations, the controls were ineffective as they failed to verify the accuracy of the declarations and estimates submitted by the candidate producer groups and producer organisations with regard to the sale of transferable production. In 2013, the European Court of Auditors audited the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, the Marshal's offices of Kujawsko-Pomorskie, Lubelskie, Mazowieckie and the selected beneficiaries of the final implementation of the aid for pre-recognized producer groups. In its audit conclusions, the ECA showed that the Polish authorities did not implement effective control procedures to prevent irregularities resulting from too general selection criteria and control requirements adopted and the lack of appropriate guidance at national and Union level. The deficiencies concerned the approval of investigation plans for the recognition of producer groups, the non-compliance with eligibility criteria and particularly artificially created groups, the failure to verify the correctness of the technical infrastructure investigation plans, machinery, equipment available to the members of the group, the non-verification of the reasonableness of investment costs, particularly when purchasing by the members of the group. In addition, the auditors did not find any evidence in the audited documents to verify the commercial consistency and the technical quality of the investigation plans. In the explanatory memorandum, they provided an indication of the time shown for the return on planned investments. Thus, in the 14 plans of the investigation, the average period of return for investments exceeded 40 years and in the extreme case 307 years. Of the 17 controlled groups in 12, the auditors questioned the transactions concluded in relation to investments between the group and its members or its closely related entities. In 11 groups 49 transactions concerned land, buildings, used machinery and equipment. In view of the results of these audits of the European Union audit bodies and the failure by the European Commission to accept the arguments presented by the Polish side, the European Commission has committed the Polish party to take corrective action under the financial assistance mechanism to the pre-accepted producer groups in accordance with the Commission's guidelines and recommendations. The corrective action plan in the financial assistance mechanism was sent to the pre-established fruit and vegetable producer groups on 5 January ub. As of 2015, and provided for its implementation by the end of 2015. Unfortunately, this plan wasn't left. As a result, on 8 January we asked the European Commission to extend the period of implementation of the corrective action plan until the end of October 2016. The plan is mainly implemented by the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture, which has carried out actions to improve the procedures for carrying out the training of workers, to carry out proper checks on the current proposals for reviewing and re-assessment of the status of initial recognition and to verify the correctness of financial assistance to pre-accepted producer groups. Of the 204 groups and organisations of fruit and vegetable producers whose initial recognition status has been assessed by the Directors of the Branches of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture, the recognition was confirmed in 153 cases, and in 51 cases on the basis of documents obtained from marshals, the correctness of the initial recognition status of those entities has not been confirmed. For seven operators, the Marshals issued decisions to withdraw initial recognition, one entity is prepared to take action to withdraw initial recognition, and the cases of 43 entities negatively verified by the Agency are currently at the Agricultural Market Agency, because the competences of the Marshal's offices were transferred to the Agricultural Market Agency in the middle of last year and are subject to re-examination. In line with the European Commission's suggestion to re-check the correctness of the recognition, the Agency will select a sample of 5% to be checked. those in which preliminary recognition has not been contested. [[♪ The bell ♪]] So, let me recap...

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u104	Thank you very much. And I'm asking the Minister to answer.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u105	As far as re-checks are concerned, they must not, of course, affect the continuity of the State. But we must bear in mind that the European Commission has excluded more than EUR 55 million from the 2009-2012 period by decision of Poland, while at the end stage there is a procedure for 20122013, where the exemption concerns EUR 115 million. These are powerful amounts, although it must be acknowledged that in 2005 and 2015 fruit and vegetable groups received more than PLN 7 billion. Thus, as far as the legality of these checks is concerned, these checks are based on post-control requests which have already been in place since 2010. Of course, if there are no irregularities found as a result of this verification, the groups have no reason to fear anything. On the other hand, the Polish State bears huge costs due to these irregularities. The Ministry of Agriculture has requested the Supreme Audit Chamber to carry out a review of the causes of this situation, as this requires checking many institutions, including marshal offices, which are not subject to the Ministry of Agriculture, and there must be done by a specialised body, which is the Supreme Audit Chamber, of course, if it takes such control. So to answer these questions, what are the reasons for this situation, I would say that, of course, only a full explanation by the Supreme Audit Chamber, by the state control authorities, can lead to the determination of these reasons. Probably in each case of any group in which irregularities have occurred, these causes may vary. In general, the European Commission's auditors concluded that the reasons were in the recognition of groups that should not be recognised and then in the payment of grants to groups that overestimated costs in the opinion of the European Commission's auditors and with their own members concluded agreements on excessive costs, but, of course, we cannot say that this happened in specific groups, because these checks, [[♪ The bell ♪]] both ours and the possible Supreme Audit Chamber are still in progress and a summary will only be possible in the future. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u191	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, I have the honour to submit to the High House a report on the work of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on Explosives for Civil Use and certain other laws. As stated in the explanatory memorandum, the purpose of this law is to implement in the national legal order the Directives on explosives for civil use and the two Directives on pyrotechnic articles. Let me remind you that this is precisely the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 12 June 2013 on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to the making available on the market of pyrotechnic articles, the Directive of 26 February 2014 on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to the making available on the market and the control of explosives intended for civil use and the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 establishing, in accordance with the Directive of the European Community, the European Parliament and of the Council, a system for the traceability of pyrotechnic articles. The implementation of these Directives requires the amendment of the Act of 21 June 2002 on explosives intended for civil use and amendments to four other laws, i.e. the Act of 22 June 2001 on the pursuit of an economic activity carried out in the field of the manufacture and marketing of explosives, weapons, ammunition and military and police-purpose products and technology, i.e. the concession law, the Act of 28 October 2002 on liability of collective entities for acts prohibited under penalty, the Act of 16 November 2006 on tax and the Law of 9 June 2011 on geological and mining law. Work on the draft law was carried out within the framework of the subcommittee set up on 9 and 10 March and during the meeting of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development on 15 March this year. 27 amendments were introduced during the work. The amendments tabled concerned in particular the clarification of the provisions contained in the draft law. Each of these amendments had a positive opinion of both the Ministry of Development and the Parliamentary Legislative Office. Representatives of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection also participated in the work of the committee and the subcommittee. Finally, I would like to thank all the members of the committee, and in particular the members of the subcommittee appointed to work on this law, and representatives of the Ministry of Development, the Minister for his personal involvement, representatives of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, the Parliamentary Legislative Office. I hope that, as during the committee's work, the proposed records will be accepted by representatives of all clubs. On behalf of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, I ask that the report be adopted and that this resolution be adopted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u192	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. I will ask Mr Antoni Duda to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u193	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club with regard to the government draft law amending the Act on Explosives for Civil Use and some other laws, print No 275. The purpose of this law is to implement in the national legal order the directive on explosives intended for civil use and two Directives on pyrotechnic materials, i.e. Directive 2013/29/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 12 June 2013 on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to the making available on the market of pyrotechnic articles and Directive 2014/28/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 February 2014 on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to the making available on the market and control of explosives for civil uses and Commission Implementing Directive 2014/58/EU of 16 April 2014 establishing, in accordance with Directive 2007/23/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council, a system for the traceability of pyrotechnic articles. Implementation of the above mentioned provisions In particular, the directives require the amendment of the Law of 21 June 2002 on explosives intended for civil use, which is a fundamental legal act governing this issue. In addition, it is necessary to draw up two regulations of the Minister of Economic Affairs for the adoption of a law containing the provisions implementing the Directives in question on specific safety requirements and procedures for assessing the conformity of explosives intended for civil use and pyrotechnic articles. In addition, it is necessary to amend four other laws: the Act of 22 June 2001 on the execution of economic activities in the field of the manufacture and marketing of explosives, weapons, ammunition and military and police goods and technology, the Act of 28 October 2002 on the liability of collective entities for acts prohibited under penalty, the Act of 16 November 2006 on the levy and the Act of 9 June 2011 on geological and mining law. The proposed amendments to the Act include the following main problem areas: obligations of manufacturers, importers and distributors of explosives intended for civil use and pyrotechnic articles, and, in the case of explosives intended for civil use, additionally authorised representatives of the manufacturer, related to ensuring compliance of those devices with essential safety requirements, as well as informing users of those materials and pyrotechnic articles of the conditions of safe use; obligations of the modified units related to the designation of pyrotechnic articles by means of registration numbers and the keeping of a register of registration numbers given; rules for the control of movements of explosives intended for civil use and ammunition, including movements by transit of such materials through the territory of the Republic of Poland; surveillance of the market for explosives intended for civil use. Control tasks for these materials used in mining, which represents up to 98% of the market for explosives for civil use in Poland, have been and will continue to be performed by the Higher Mining Authority. Another area is sanctions for economic operators operating in the area of these pyrotechnic materials and articles for failing to comply with the requirements of the Act. When amending the Act on Explosives for Civil Use in connection with the implementation of the Directives, amendments were also introduced to remove the ambiguities in the existing legislation governing the issue, identified in the course of their practical application by public administrations and economic operators involved in the acquisition, storage and use of explosives for civil use. In summary, the proposed changes to the rules are intentional and contribute to safeguarding the interests of the Polish State, contributing to the improvement of the level of safety of citizens and at the same time to users of explosives intended for civil use and pyrotechnic articles. They also contribute to the elimination of various obstacles to intra-Community trade. The Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the provisions of the amended Act in print No 275 and recommend its adoption by the Sejm. Thank you for your attention. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u199	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The regulation of the market for explosives for civil uses is extremely specialised. It aims to put this market under scrutiny by state authorities and to lay down criminal and administrative sanctions for infringements of those rules. The purpose of the regulation is to define procedures to increase safety at the design, manufacture, use and transport stage of explosives. The revision implements the provisions of the three directives of the European Union from 2013 and 2014. The basic principles for regulating the market for explosives for civil uses, including pyrotechnic articles and ammunition, should be mentioned at the outset, as pyrotechnic articles and ammunition are also, of course, explosives. First, an economic operator must be authorised to manufacture and store explosives, move and use them, which is governed by the concession law, i.e. the Act of 22 June 2001 on the pursuit of economic activities in the field of the manufacture and marketing of explosives, weapons, ammunition and military or police-purpose products and technology. Unlicensed activities and concessions constitute a criminal offence. Secondly, the design and manufacture of explosives must comply with the safety requirements laid down in the legislation. The manufacturer shall be required to obtain an EU type-examination certificate issued by a notified body authorised for that purpose which examines both the technical documentation of the product and the sample of the product. The manufacturer shall, after obtaining the certificate, draw up a declaration of EU conformity which shall entitle him to affix the CE marking on the material. The manufacturer in serial production is to obtain products identical to the sample that has been certified. The manufacturer is also obliged to submit instructions for use and information to the product. The breach of security requirements is to be subject to an administrative penalty. Thirdly, the carriage of explosives across the state border requires authorisation and the absence of such explosives is punishable by criminal sanctions. Fourthly, the storage, possession or use of explosives for civil uses shall be subject to control by the authorities of the State and the restriction or prohibition of controls shall be punishable by a fine. The Modern Club recommends that the Sejm adopt the draft law as set out in the report of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development with several amendments. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u215	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Government's draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded health care and certain other laws with the draft implementing act is an extremely important project addressed to senior citizens of 75+. The entry into force of this law will enable the oldest members of our society to access some of the free medicines needed in their daily lives. The government bill not only implements the assumptions contained in the exposé by Prime Minister Beata Szydło, but above all reduces the spending of seniors on medicines. In line with the draft law, the cost of medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses and the medical devices concerned will be borne by the State budget. In accordance with existing regulations, recipients who are 75 years of age and do not have additional rights have the same access to medicines reimbursed as other recipients. Authorisation of the recipient after the age of 75 to supply medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses free of charge and medical devices included in the list referred to in Article. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Refund Act is implemented only on the basis of age criterion, which is confirmed by patient information. The basis for receiving free medicines or special-purpose foodstuffs will be a prescription with the letter S issued by a primary healthcare doctor, a primary healthcare nurse or a doctor who has the right to pursue a profession who has ceased to pursue the profession and has issued a prescription for himself or for a spouse, initial straight line and siblings. In this context, it may be necessary to consider whether or not to allow geriatric doctors to prescribe prescriptions. The presence of polypragma among older people makes it seem appropriate for geriatric doctors to issue a prescription. However, they speak from the Ministry's arguments that, due to the lack of an IT system, such a solution is not possible today. Perhaps in the future, systemic solutions will have to be considered, which would also allow geriatrics and other specialists to prescribe medicines refunded in accordance with the law. The draft law implements one of the provisions of the Constitution, according to which the State should take special care of the elderly. The draft law provides for the granting of free medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses and medical devices listed by the Minister of Health to this particular group of beneficiaries aged 75+. When analysing the bill, we need to consider both its negative and its positive aspects. Some of the allegations against the draft law may be repeated in favour of the Sejm Analysis Office, which in its opinion emphasizes that the law focuses only on one social group. It is true that the law does not cover the scope of other entities referred to in Article. Article 68 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 and 3 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, e.g. Disabled or children who certainly also deserve exceptional state care and financial assistance implemented by the State. However, it is more striking to me that the Act omits other older people. Special aid from the state and, above all, systemic changes in the area of senior policy are also expected by seniors 60+ or 70+. Expectations related to assistance for older people both the Kukiz Club of Posel and seniors far exceed the scope of the contested law. It is fair to say that we would prefer to extend the establishment of the bill to a larger group of recipients. However, we are aware of the budgetary possibilities set out in the 2016 budget. We hope that next year's budget will make it possible to extend the group of beneficiaries of free medicines. There may also be doubts about the provision in the draft law that the Minister of Health will decide to what extent entitled persons will be able to use free medicines. This decision will therefore be linked to the needs of a specific recipient, arising both from the state of health and from the financial situation of eligible persons. The lack of specific criteria for the selection of medicines that will be included in the Minister of Health notice in the new law raises our concerns. However, we trust that, according to assurances, drugs that form the basis of the limit or are included in the limit, i.e. currently being for a fee of PLN 3.20, will not be taken into account first. We believe that the S-list will include medicines that today require seniors to co-pay at least 30%, because only such a change can clearly reduce drug spending for people over the age of 75. Among the advantages of the bill is the fact that it assumes greater freedom for the patient to choose medicines, foods for particular nutritional uses and medical devices other than those prescribed on the reception desk, but which do not create therapeutic differences. This is certainly a record that is in line with social expectations. It is also valuable that, as the Minister of Health assures, more expensive medicines are also to be refunded. It is with buying these drugs that seniors have the biggest problem. In the explanatory memorandum of the contested law, the government explains that one of its basic objectives is to prevent the phenomenon of non-buying medicines by seniors. The Act in this context is intended to provide senior citizens with access to medicines which were previously not economically possible. It is clear that older people often suffer from many underlying diseases and that the medicines they need to use are a significant burden on their monthly budget. The bill, which is so desirable by environments bringing together older people, allows seniors, regardless of their property status, to comprehensive treatment, and thus to the, desirable by the whole society, longer functioning in the health of the oldest of its representatives. This law promotes the improvement of health of the oldest Poles, which is definitely a valuable element shaping holistic senior policy in Poland. This project, which enables seniors to live better lives, is also fulfilling some of my personal expectations of the government. The longer life of seniors, improved quality are undoubtedly the advantages resulting from the implementation of this law. Undoubtedly, it is also important that the Act provides for a gradual increase in refund amounts of 15%. a year, as well as a gradual extension of the free list of medicines for seniors. As the Kukiz Posel Club15, we had more expectations about this bill, but we hope that this is only the beginning of important changes in the context of senior policy. The Kukiz Group will vote in favour of law 75+.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u304	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on a draft law on special solutions related to the organisation of the visit of His Holiness Pope Francis to the Republic of Poland and the World Youth Days - Kraków 2016. At its 14th meeting on 16 March 2016, the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs for consideration. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, after considering this proposal, asks the High House to adopt this proposal. I would like to say that as a result of the work carried out in the Committee on Home Affairs and Administration, nine amendments were tabled to this draft. Of these nine amendments, seven amendments were accepted and two amendments were rejected. The most serious amendment relates to Article. 4 of this law, where, in a way, it seems appropriate to resolve the matter related to the overall organisation of World Youth Days and all these issues concerning the development of this and ensuring the order and security of the participants of the World Youth Days. In the original submission there was a formulation which, as if the main burden of this responsibility had been placed on the church site. In this case, the article also moves this part of this responsibility to the voivode, which, in agreement with the organizer of the World Youth Days, i.e. the archdiocese of Krakow, will prepare all these security documents. The next amendment and discussion concerned the article. 11. Here was the proposal to set a deadline for the voivodeship for all these medical security plans. We thought that since the bill had a uniform time to draw up all documents - this is such a limited time, 15 May - there is no need to enter this date here, because it could also complicate at a later stage the correction of these medical plans. Second or second amendment in Article. 15 also addressed the question of the deadline and referred to the issue of medical security. Finally, what has attracted great attention is the amendments related to the article. Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1. This is an important issue during the World Youth Days: communication issues and a provision that addresses issues related to the identification of roads recommended to participants in the World Youth Days. This will be available not only to the organiser, but also to all participants and to the media. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 24 we added points 4 and 5 which refer to the cost of transporting His Holiness Pope Francis from Poland to Rome. This amendment was accepted by adding points 4 and 5. Finally, in the article. 25, and this is a very important issue, issues have been settled regarding the Public Procurement Act and what concerns matters relating to the provision of services, the carrying out of works related to the organisation of World Youth Days and other purchases, so that the Public Procurement Act does not paralyze these purchases. Because it is easy to imagine that by applying the current thresholds relating to public procurement or deadlines, we would have had such a situation, for example. It would be difficult to make purchases of ambulances, because it would be an amount exceeding the value of 130 thousand. Euros. And finally, art. 29, in which we have also made a change. Namely, this concerns issues related to the installation by telecommunications operators, in connection with the organization of World Youth Days, in the city of Cracow and in all areas where the pope will be staying, temporary radio installations, various communication devices to improve communication among participants. The idea was not to obtain special permits for the installation of these temporary devices. And finally, art. 30, which states that the Minister for National Defence may, after consulting the competent voivodeship, provide support for the proper organisation of the World Youth Days in the areas of infrastructure, logistics, transport, communications, security and public order and medical and sanitary protection. These were the amendments that were accepted. An extensive discussion was also triggered by the article. Point 36 (3) which refers to purely - it would seem - technical, but concerns costs related to the drainage of the land on which the youth will reside. On a good note, we left time for second reading to also settle this issue at second reading. Thank you very much, Marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u326	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present on behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights a report on the Government's draft law on the prevention of sexual crime threats. At its 9th meeting on 27 January, the Sejm sent the draft law to the committee. The committee met on 9 February and 15 March 2016. An extraordinary subcommittee was also set up to examine the draft law. I had the privilege of chairing this subcommittee. The subcommittee held four meetings. Written opinions for the bill were presented by the Minister of Interior and Administration, the Ombudsman, the Ombudsman of the Rights of the Child, the National Judicial Council, the Attorney General, the Data Protection Officer General, the Office for Sejm Analysis and the Helsinki Foundation for Human Rights. In accordance with the authority given in the submission, representatives of the Ministry of Justice presented the position of the government during the work in committee and subcommittee. In addition, representatives of the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration, the Chief of Police, the Ombudsman, the Ombudsman, the Prosecutor General and the General Data Protection Officer took part in the work of the committee and the subcommittee. According to Art. 1 of the draft law sets out specific protection measures against sexual crime threats. Such offences are, with exemptions as set out in Article. 2 laws, crimes against sexual freedom listed in chapter XXV of the Penal Code. Special protection measures under the Act are: firstly, the Register of Criminals on the Sexual Background, which consists of two separate bases: a register with limited access and a public register, further, the obligations of employers and other organisers in relation to activities related to education, education, recreation, treatment or care of minors, and finally the identification of places of special risk of sexual crime. During the work of the committee and the subcommittee, the view was widely expressed on the appropriateness of this legislative initiative. It involves preventing and combating sexual crime, especially against children and young people. All the regulations of the project have been discussed in a substantive and systematic manner three times: twice during the work of the subcommittee and once during the work of the committee. The opinions submitted and during the discussions raised doubts about some of the institutions and regulations of the project, in particular the existence and scope of the public register. As a result of the discussion and the amendments notified, much of the doubts were eliminated or clearly mitigated. The challenge will be to create a new electronic system for register keeping, which has affected the extension of the vacathio legis period until 1 October 2017. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights, after considering the draft law on the prevention of sexual crime, asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law as proposed in the committee's report in print no 330. At the same time, the committee in accordance with the Rule. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm present 29 minority proposals to the applicants in the persons of Mrs Zofia Czernow and Ms Kamila Gasiuk-Pihowicz. I would like to thank you very warmly for the constructive cooperation between ladies and gentlemen, as well as all the experts and participants in committee and subcommittee meetings. In the event of specific questions, I shall remain at the disposal of the High House.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u327	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear ten-minute statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Daniel Milewski, Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u328	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Every now and then, the media informs us, and at the same time saddens us with such reports, of further cases of sexual crimes. Every crime, and in particular one that violates the security of such a delicate and intimate sphere as sexuality, deserves decisive action by the authorities of the state. It is therefore undeniable that when Prime Minister Beata Szydło's initiative to adopt a law on the prevention of sexual crime risks came from the government, the project gained acceptance and support of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. This law is another step forward in the implementation of the policy package of legal solutions to protect the youngest. This electoral promise, which we are fulfilling, had previously included, among others. The only reason not to take children away from their parents is because their homes are poor. Although this example and this law apply to other aspects, they refer to the most sensitive spheres of life that affect the development of the youngest inhabitants of our country and their happy childhood. Just after the media described the story of a young woman from Siemiatic since a child imprisoned and raped by her father, Prime Minister Donald Tusk announced a policy of zero tolerance for pedophiles in 2008. A revised year later, the Penal Code introduced the concept of chemical castration, or treatment of paedophiles with means to reduce potency. A mandatory castration for paedophile rapists who served time was to be carried out in specially prepared psychiatric hospitals. However, these, which were opened in the light of the cameras, had rather an image dimension and had more impact on the indicators of political support than on statistics on sexual offences. This was, in our opinion, only a partial solution to the problem. However, my intention is not to criticize, because any action that improves safety and protects against paedophiles deserves to be appreciated and is a solid brick that builds a wall that protects victims of sexual crimes. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully shares the views of applicants and acknowledges that sexual crime is a growing problem. In particular, recent years with the development of IT and ICT technologies bring new risks in this area. The ease of communication, the anonymity of contacts and the universality of access to means of communication, while not being able to ensure effective supervision, make it possible to reach each of the potential victims and become the main field of action of these crimes. This is also supported by the increasing anonymity of life and the breakdown of control functions of social environments. In atomized societies where escape to privacy replaces a model of life in the community, crime becomes easier and thus the risk of being a victim of crime increases. This risk appears to be very high in particular with regard to children, and it should be stressed that the damage caused to them is often irreversible. This problem has been widely recognised and, at present, in many countries and in the fora of many international organisations, measures are being taken to implement measures to protect against sexual crime that are appropriate to the situation. Today, there is an increasingly common belief that, in order to combat sexual crime, the state cannot stop with reactive actions limited to detecting and punishing perpetrators of crimes already committed. It is assumed that there are currently two models of strategies for dealing with sex offenders. I have already mentioned the model of clinical work implemented in Poland in recent years, which emphasizes the treatment of perpetrators both during the time of their punishment and after their release. It is used, among others. in the Netherlands and Italy. The second model, in our opinion more effective, is the protection of the local community, the community protection model. Its main feature is the supervision of the perpetrators who have left the penal facility. This supervision shall be carried out by means of a set of tools, including by means of registers or verification of applicants for employment in educational and caring establishments where children are or may be present. This model is used, among others. in the United States and the United Kingdom. There are countries, for example. France, which use the combination of these methods of combating sexual violence. The most effective way for pedophiles is for Americans. The names, images and residence of the perpetrators are revealed, and the recidivists are isolated for life. In the UK, they are under constant control, and in Germany, so-called serial pedophiles are used. Preventive custody. Therapists point out that in Poland a pedophile can easily hire as a janitor in kindergarten or guardian at a Boy Scout camp. It doesn't work if you've ever been punished for pedophilia before. The fight against sexual abuse has been focused from the beginning on helping the victim and her loved ones. On the other hand, very often after the sentence has been served, the executioners are again guilty of assault. However, the events of recent years show that it is also important to focus on developing an effective policy against sex offenders. In the current state of law, there is insufficient resources to enable the perpetrators of sexual offences to be effectively controlled, and this often results in more similar crimes being committed by them. There are also no mechanisms effectively preventing contact with children, in particular sex offenders. It is therefore necessary to introduce the proposed law. It is the beginning of such a comprehensive, not only media war against paedophiles. On the one hand, the sex offender register will act as a warning to the public, and on the other hand it will be an additional ailment for the convicted. It reveals a number of personal data of criminals: information about penalties imposed, addresses of check-in and stay, as well as the image of the perpetrator. As Members of the Parliamentary Club, we believe that access to such information can significantly increase the safety of minors, particularly in areas where the authorities of the state or local government carry out activities related to education, education, care or recreation. The information obtained from the register is a valuable source not only for law enforcement or justice, but also for all, including public authorities, whose actions may somehow be related to minors. Sexual crimes, as a particularly shameful kind of crimes committed to the detriment of minors and often resulting in permanent defects and disturbances of victims in the sphere of emotional life and in the sphere of private life, deserve restrictive penalization. On the other hand, the welfare of minors demands special protection, ensuring that their contact with potential sex offenders is kept to a minimum during education and development. The bill is groundbreaking because there were no similar measures in Poland to protect society against sexual crime. The Act on the Prevention of Sexual Crime is intended to protect citizens from perpetrators, especially those who are more likely to be victims of this type of crime because of their age. On behalf of our parliamentary environment, I would also like to thank you for the fruitful work that has been done in the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, and especially in the subcommittee, which was a field of broad consultation. I would like to thank the guests for their many expert comments, which have enabled them to make valuable detailed amendments, thanks to which they have made the result of years of work in this field. A relatively long period of vacathio legis of the proposed law should also be noted. It will allow the establishment of a register based on it, as well as the organisation of other instruments it introduces. The Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, in support of this bill, agrees with the applicants that the solutions proposed therein should contribute to improving citizens' sense of security and, thanks to the preventive mechanisms introduced, also reduce the risk of sexual abuse that minors could face. In view of the arguments put forward, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the adoption of this law. Thank you very much.

The amendments of the Civic Platform ensure that, after taking them into account, this project could count on our support. Otherwise, we will inform about our position after voting on the amendments of the Civic Platform. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u332	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! The bill deals with very important social issues - preventing and combating sexual crime, in particular the protection of minors from such crimes. This is a very important project. The practice proves that the perpetrators of sex crimes against children often return to the crime despite previous convictions and that the sentenced sentence does not fulfil a sufficient preventive role. The project introduces additional measures to protect citizens, in particular children, from sexual crime. Sexual offences are international in nature and only the joint effort of all countries can be effectively combated. In Poland, the Penal Code has been repeatedly amended in order to increase the protection of sexual freedom, especially children. The existing institutions of criminal law make it possible to achieve to a large extent the objectives set out in this proposal. This is demonstrated by the statistics, which are also mentioned in the explanatory memorandum to the project, which say that sexual crime is decreasing from year to year. I do not mean to say that the situation is satisfactory and there is no need to take new action. Conversely, any initiative aimed at reducing the scale of the phenomenon deserves a thorough analysis. The creation of a Register of Criminals on the Sexual Background committed with particular cruelty and the imposition on employers and other organisers of activities related to education, education, recreation, treatment of minors or care for them of the obligation to verify the criminal past of applicants for employment is a legitimate and necessary action. Such a task meets a limited-access register, which, due to the very wide range of information about the convicted person and a wide catalogue of authorised information from that register, allows the purpose of the project to be achieved. There are limited access criminal records in France and the United Kingdom. Unlike a limited-access register, the institution of the public register raises serious constitutional doubts, and the fact that no European country has a public register makes it necessary to reflect on the validity of its introduction. The draft law on the establishment of a public register does not adequately protect victims of sexual offences and in particular children. The publication of personal data and other information on the perpetrator may lead to the identification of the victim in the local environment and thus to secondary victimisation of the victim. It is necessary to protect children or minor victims of sexual offences against identification. Wide access to the register can paradoxically reduce the number of sexual offences reported by victims in fear of being identified and branded. Yet, it is something completely different - an increase in the detection of these crimes. The proposed regulation on the public register constitutes a major interference in the constitutionally protected sphere of rights and it is necessary to assess the respect of the principle of proportionality referred to in Article. 31th Constitution. These are essential standards in the rule of law and we want to live in the state of law. I would like to point out that the extensive sections of Andrzej Sakowicz's publication on the register of sex offenders used in the explanatory memorandum are selective, as they ignore the author's very important request to introduce a register in Poland. I quote: access to it should be limited, left to the representatives of the judiciary, law enforcement and others - I will not mention, the end of the quote. I read the full text of this publication. I very much regret that the authors of the justification have omitted such an important part of this publication. In conclusion, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club has tabled amendments and then minority proposals aimed at abandoning the establishment of a public register. I appeal to Members of all political options to support minority proposals. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u336	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Poselski Club in relation to the draft law on the prevention of sexual crime threats, print No 189, following the report of the committee in print No 330. Ladies and gentlemen! Every crime against a child is a crime, and a sexual crime is a monstrous crime. We must undoubtedly protect all children. In principle, any legislation aimed at improving security for the general public or for the selected group is desirable. However, for a reason I used the words "in principle." Legislation must introduce solutions which will have a real effect in the area concerned. This is all the more important in the areas covered by this project, namely on citizens' safety and children's safety. Here all effective action is undoubtedly simply necessary. Unfortunately, today we are talking about a law that will bring good solutions, but, more importantly, it can harm many people. But that's what we're about to hear. At first, I'm going to focus on those elements that are going in the right direction. We note that the provisions of the Act provide that prior to having a relationship with a person, or before allowing another person to do other activities related to education, education, recreation, treatment or care of minors, employers or other organisers in the field of such activities are obliged to obtain information as to whether the data of that person are included in the register with limited access. It's the right thing to do. It will help to improve the safety of children placed under care. It can also be assumed that a secret register is a good solution, which will allow the verification of applicants for work with children and will allow a quick explanation of the status of the person indicated by the relevant services, that it is a good solution to inform the police of the whereabouts which the law introduces for the perpetrator of the register. I'm sure the police will use it to prevent it, which will increase the level of security. On this, however, solutions that can be said to be going in the right direction end. The worse ones start. On many occasions, I have wondered how to balance the good and the bad. I have always come to the conclusion that this is a question of how much the purpose justifies the means. How much further suffering will this bill cause for those who have already been harmed? How much will this bill affect those who have the misfortune of being close to convicted degenerates? How much of the country's role is comprehensive security, and how much should the state cede this obligation on its citizens? I must say, these consequences are difficult to accept. I am sad to say, too, that today we have failed to convince the government to change its mind. We have failed to persuade the government to compromise and balance the measures contained in the bill, and we will find there two solutions which, in our view, are disqualifying. The first solution is a map of particularly dangerous sites published on websites. We appreciate your good intentions, but no criminal with online access to this map will be haunted in these places. On the contrary, it will move to those areas that are designated as safe. That's where he's gonna hunt the victims. Such a map, yes, should be created, but to plan preventive measures. These preventive measures would indeed improve safety. A map could be available at the request of individuals. However, the publication of an online map will even increase the sense of danger in those areas that the map considers dangerous. This is of course not the only negative effect of such a map, but in our opinion this is the most important one. The second controversial solution is the public register. We see good intentions here, too, but, ladies and gentlemen, it's like with good intentions that hell is paved. These good intentions translated into the project records can bring hell for the victims and for the close criminals included in the register. Why are we so judgmental? Well, according to the available data, about 80% sex crimes are committed in families by people close to them, by people known to them. Therefore, the data of the perpetrators included in the register will result in the identification of the victims and the marking of the close perpetrators, i.e., first, the victims and, second, those who are absolutely innocent in this situation. These people will be stigmatized. We are all aware of what this means for a child attending school when he is stigmatized with some terrible story, which means to God the spirit of the guilty ones who are close to him to mark them as a degenerate family. Such stigmatising, ladies and gentlemen, is simply wicked. It must also be said that there are very few registers in the world that are completely open, and only in some U.S. states. Experts clearly say that stigmatized criminals become hunters, change their appearance, place of residence, are not traceable, are not recognized or controlled. They live on the outskirts, and they're out in completely different areas, threatening the lives of minors. It's hard to accept the cost. At the end of this calculation we must also honestly mention that this is a simple way to provoke fear and aggression on the part of parents, which will be difficult to master. It's the way we find the wrong names, the wrong photographs, the wrong faces, the wrong people. It is a simple road to public lynching, which in the state of law should be unacceptable. During committee meetings, we identified other areas as those that could be better achieved, but the two points above are most important to us. Now I'm going to tell you what's not in this bill. The authors of the bill had two goals: prevention and stigmatization of sex offenders. The experience and opinions of experts, also presented during committee meetings, show that only one of these paths, precisely prevention, but linked to therapy, with the continuous monitoring of these criminals, can have the effect of reducing the amount of sexual crimes committed. It is a pity that the government has not attempted such a holistic one, taking into account the above-mentioned demands for this difficult subject. Ladies and gentlemen! The modern has tabled amendments to the minority. For the reasons that I have just mentioned today, I appeal once again to the Government: Ladies and gentlemen, I ask you very much to examine our amendments and to see them care for the victims and the innocent. Back them up. There are records in this act that significantly improve the safety of Polish children and other people, but there are, unfortunately, also those that will cause harm. The goal cannot justify the means that hurt victims once again and innocent people, because there is absolutely no humanity in this. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u337	Thank you very much. Now I ask for the voice of Mr Krzysztof Paszyk from the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u338	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present the position of the club regarding the draft act on the prevention of sexual crime threats, prints no. 189 and 330. The bill is based on three key pillars. The first is the introduction of a register of sex offenders, the second is the imposition on employers and other organisers of responsibilities in the field of education, education, treatment or care of minors and the third is the identification of places where sexual offences are particularly at risk. The idea of creating a sex offenders register is right and necessary. However, the Polish People's Party shares concerns about the content of the law expressed by the Polish Sex Society. I will quote a passage from this position: The Polish Sex Society is of the opinion that no registers of sex offenders of a public nature should be created. I'm sorry, Firstly, the long-term evaluation of the effectiveness of public registers operating in other countries clearly and consistently shows that they do not contribute to a decrease in the level of sexual recidivism. This means that public registers are completely ineffective in reducing the risk of sexual crime. Secondly, the results of this evaluation also show that the existence of registers does not result in a reduction in the overall level of sexual crime. This means that public registers do not act as a deterrent either. I'm sorry, Thirdly, the existence of such a register significantly reduces the possibility of sexual offenders being treated. This is because for the perpetrators to appear in the register, especially those who will be there for life (the perpetrators referred to in Art. 106a of the Criminal Code), there will be no prospect of a return to society, which will significantly reduce the possibility of their therapy and rehabilitation. This means that the existence of public registers hinders the prevention of sexual crime. Fourthly, the existence of public records of sex offenders not only does not prevent sex crimes but also does not reduce the level of social fear associated with such crimes. There is a counter-dependence here - the existence of such a register increases the level of social anxiety associated with sexual crimes. It should therefore be added that the identification of places of special threat referred to in the Act will increase this fear. The project under investigation assumes the existence of two registers: a register with limited access and a public register. Draft law in Article. In spite of restricting the scope of the information received, 12 (6) shall grant access to the register with limited access to employers and other organisers before admitting a person to an activity related to education, education, recreation, treatment or care of minors. The question arises as to whether, in connection with the expert opinion just quoted, we are not dealing with a very wide range of entities having access to the full register. As I said at the outset, the PSL Parliamentary Club is in favour of creating a register, but it is worth listening to specialists on this issue, and they are in the position that the existence of non-public sex offenders registers is an important element of the anti-sexual crime system. Of course, there is a need to organize the therapy of perpetrators, probation and post-penitentiary supervision. With regard to the changes made during the committee's work, it is to be welcomed that it has been introduced: firstly, to extend the scope of the offences excluded in Article. 2 of the proposed law, in particular by removing criminal offences covered by compulsory registration in the Register of Crimes referred to in Article. 202 § 4a-4c, as in the case of these offences the entry in the register could be too severe for the perpetrator; secondly, the amendment to Article. 6 concerning the deletion of the entry in the register of persons against whom proceedings on the basis of amnesty have been definitively discontinued; thirdly, amendments to Article. 9, increasing the powers of the court in the context of making entries in the register; fourthly, restricting the transitional provisions of the circle of persons who have committed sexual offences before the entry into force of this Act. In this respect, there is serious doubt as to the compatibility of the proposed Article. 27 by law, because according to the Roman principle of nulla poena sine lege, which is in force under criminal law in Poland, it is not possible to apply a penalty to a criminal that was not in force during the commission of the crime, and in the context of the penalty an entry in the register should be considered. In conclusion, the direction of the creation of a sex offenders register should be positively assessed, which could significantly improve the safety of citizens. However, the creation of a public register can to a large extent be a stigmatization of close perpetrators and, as already expressed by the previous speakers, places where these dramatic events have taken place, associated with such prohibited acts. It is clear that the task of the State is to protect citizens and citizens from the dangers of convicted unscathed improvements, but we must also bear in mind reducing the barriers posed to criminals that prevent them from returning to society. However, this does not change the fact that the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in the position that the law on the prevention of sexual crime is needed in Poland, and thus we will support the proposed law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u352	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! At its 13th meeting on 9 March, the Sejm referred the draft law to the committee for consideration. The committee was held on 16 March. It was attended by representatives of the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office with the Director of Prof. Małgorzata Manowska, representatives of the Ministry of Justice and a representative of the National Judicial Council. The applicants were represented by Mr Michał Wójcik. The National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office was established by the Law of 23 January 2009. The Minister of Justice is responsible for the supervision of the school. According to Art. 2 (2) 1 point 3 of the Act is the task of a national school to conduct a judicial application and a prosecutor's application, the purpose of which is to obtain the necessary knowledge and practical preparation by applicants for the appointment of a judge, judicial assistant, prosecutor and prosecutor of the prosecutor's office. The Commission considered it appropriate, as intended, to temporarily extend the duration of the two-stage initial training model, consisting of a 12-month general application and a subsequent 30-month judicial or prosecutorial application. The solutions adopted last year by the Act on the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office raised concerns about the effectiveness of training candidates for judges and prosecutors. The extension of the existing form of training will allow for the announcement of recruitment in 2016 for the general application, while at the same time giving time for extensive discussion and the preparation of necessary and well-thought-out changes. In view of the fact that the national school is subject to the supervision of the Minister of Justice, the committee also supports the proposal that the head of the school be appointed and, under certain conditions, also cancelled by the Minister of Justice. The recognition of the committee also found the proposed amendments to the Law of 27 July 2001. The Law on the System of General Courts, in particular that the President of the Court of Appeal should inform the Minister of Justice as early as possible if he had been informed of the intended retirement of the judge. This will improve the management of judicial posts and allow for a shift in activities relating to the formation of first assesor positions in regional courts. This is intended to allow legislative work to be undertaken in order to optimally shape the model of access to the judge's office by the graduates of the judge's application. Legislative amendments were adopted during the committee's work and the name of the law was changed in this framework. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights, after considering the draft law amending the Act on the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office and certain other laws, asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law as proposed in the committee's report in print No 331. The project assumes the training of applicants in Krakow and their practice in common courts and prosecutors' offices. The current state of law assumes that school applicants would be employed in court and district attorneys' offices, where they would perform the same duties as assistants. This is an optimal solution, because on the one hand such applicants are better prepared for the profession, and on the other hand the actions of the courts and prosecutors will be improved. It is not for nothing in the judge's assistants' environment that there is a saying that the assistant is the master of the process, because it is the assistants who largely unload the embolism at the stage of non-trial proceedings. Regardless of the above, it should be noted that the Act on the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office has been amended several times during its period of validity. The draft law on the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office is another return to solutions that have failed and have been changed in a thoughtful manner and preceded by several months of consultation by the Sejm of the previous term of office. In view of the above reservations, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party does not see the possibility of supporting the submitted project in its proposed form. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u375	Thank you. Anna Maria Siarkowska from the Kukiz club15 will now speak. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u376	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz club15 on the Act amending the Foreign Military Rules Act on the territory of the Republic of Poland and the rules for their movement through this territory. National security is a fundamental and existential value and need in the life of the nation. For you, it is a priority objective. It is also worth starting with some strategic principles in the discussion on this subject. General Carl von Clausewitz, one of the greatest theorists of war, in his centuries-old work on war, which is a biblical strategic military thought, indicates the scope of the state's means of defence. They are, of course, using modern language, operational troops, territorial defence, operational preparation of the territory for defence, civil defense and civilian support of the armed forces and allies. As we can see, allies are listed by Clausewitz in the last place among the State's means of defence. Their role is certainly important and must be taken into account in the construction of the national security of Poland, but can certainly not be considered as its basis either. Jan Nowak-Jeziorański, promoter of the Polish accession to NATO, pointed this out. He stressed that the commonly accepted safety criterion is its own defence potential, combined, of course, with alliance agreements, ensuring effective deterrent of the potential attacker. The risk of aggression must be high enough in the perception of the opponent to make it inefficient. He emphasized that Poland could only count on helping its allies if it wanted and could defend itself if it gained its own ability to scare away the attacker. These words, this strategic science remains valid until today. It is also expressed in the agreement of the Washington Treaty, for it is before the famous art. 5, which is an ally solidarity, is still an article. 3. It obliges the signatories of the Treaty to maintain, in addition to the collective, an individual ability to resist collective assault. For it is difficult to entrust your own safety completely to allies. It is also difficult to expect that when we are deprived of our own defensive abilities, allies will come to our aid and protect our own homeland for us. Therefore, we must not forget the need to build up our own deterrent abilities. The issue of allied support is only one of them. And that's what we need to remember. This amendment concerns the creation of a clear legal basis aimed at enabling foreign troops to carry out military operations in the territory of the Republic of Poland during peacetime. The President of the Republic of Poland is to agree to this at the request of the Minister of National Defence, with the consent of the President of the Council of Ministers. In accordance with the President's statement, it is proposed to indicate the purpose of the stay and the scope of the powers, as well as other circumstances relevant to the defence of the Republic of Poland, indicating that foreign troops are to obtain the powers which are vested in the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. The need for amendments to the current law is due to a deterioration of the security situation in our region, as also indicated in the draft amendment. Indeed, the amendment will allow Poland to make quick decisions in order to launch NATO forces, even in peacetime, but only if - it should be stressed - when Poland considers it necessary. The Kukiz Club15 will, of course, support this bill, but at the same time we note that it is not the speed of use of force and strengthening NATO that de facto depends on the level of our national security. Our level of national security depends on our own defensive abilities. We ask the High House to remember that too. Let's rebuild territorial defense and operational troops. These are the two basic pillars of Polish national security. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u382	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The draft law on the amendment of the Foreign Military Rules Act on the territory of the Republic of Poland and the rules for their movement through the Republic of Poland introduced a clear legal basis according to which foreign troops will be able to conduct military operations in the territory of the Republic of Poland other than exercises during peacetime. In practice, they will be able to support our country if it is necessary to take preventive, dissuasive or requiring the use of high readiness forces in Polish territory or in the airspace of our country. The President of the Republic of Poland, at the request of the Minister of National Defence, after obtaining the consent of the President of the Council of Ministers, is to consent to the stay in Poland of foreign troops - mainly NATO and the European Union - in the framework of strengthening the military forces of the Polish Armed Forces in military operations conducted in our country during peace. In accordance with the President's stated purpose of residence and the scope of foreign military powers, and other circumstances relevant to Poland's defence are to be indicated. It was assumed that foreign troops would be able to have the powers granted to them by the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. The narrowing of the record to the time of peace results from the fact that during the war the ability of allies to use force in Poland is regulated by art. 5 of the North Atlantic Treaty. The introduction of these regulations is necessary due to the deteriorating security situation in the vicinity of Poland and other countries of Central and Eastern Europe. A changing geopolitical situation may force actions requiring immediate support of the Polish Armed Forces by foreign troops in a situation where there would not yet be grounds for launching actions pursuant to Article. 5 of the North Atlantic Treaty. This new regulation will be of great external and internal importance. In the external dimension it shows potential aggressors that in Polish law there are no barriers to the use of NATO or European Union troops in Poland. This should deter and discourage potential opponents from taking military action in Poland, since there may be a military response from the international community at any time. As far as the internal dimension is concerned, States sending their troops to Poland within NATO or the European Union will be sure to legitimize their activities in our country. The revised act will ensure effective implementation of the preparedness action plan in Poland, which allows military operations to be carried out by high readiness forces. It is about the possibility of using rapid response forces capable of responding within 48/72 hours, which requires clear and precise procedures to be introduced into national law, which does not raise doubts about interpretation. Mr. Marshal! Polish People's Party through the bill draft. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
