ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u400	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It is my pleasure to present the position of the Modern Club. The applicant shall request the application of the exemption mechanism in the Individual Income Tax Act and in the Corporate Income Tax Act for payments made in breach of Article. 22 Law on freedom of business, i.e. cash payments. At the same time, it is proposed to amend the limit of 15 000 payments. EUR 15 000 zł. This is a level that occurs in most countries of the European Union. The applicant argues in the explanatory memorandum that the regulations proposed in the Act will have a positive effect on the transparency of transactions carried out and, above all, on the increase of fair competition in business-to-business relations. The reduction of this limit should consequently contribute to increasing the revenue of the state budget by reducing the grey economy and reducing fictional transactions. What is the situation today? Accepting payments by traders in cash in excess of 15 000 The euro can bring on them a number of additional, burdensome obligations under the Anti-Money Laundering Act. The list of obliged institutions subject to the obligations under this law was added, among others. entrepreneurs within the meaning of the Freedom of Business Act. However, an important limitation has been introduced: only those entrepreneurs are obliged to accept payments for cash goods of a value equal to or exceeding the equivalent of 15 000. the euro, including where a charge for a specific commodity is made by means of more than one operation. An entrepreneur who does not accept cash payments for goods of a value equal to or exceeding the equivalent of 15 000. The euro is not an institution bound by that law and therefore does not have the obligations imposed by that law. Therefore, it does not need to register transactions, introduce internal procedures, train employees. It is therefore not worthwhile to accept cash at the moment, which is cumbersome, and therefore many obligations and severe penalties are imposed for non-compliance with these rules. Please note that the introduction of an obligation to make or accept payments related to the economic activity carried out through the entrepreneur's bank account of more than 15 000. zł gives rise to different opinions. The reasons we have been able to learn are the following: firstly, ensuring the protection of the public interest by preventing uncontrolled financial flows, secondly, preventing tax avoidance, third case, minimising cases of failure to comply with business-to-business relations. On the other hand, there is also a critical assessment of this obligation as it reduces flexibility in business-to-business relations. Certainly, the most important positive reason for reducing the limit is to reduce the cash flow, which is less subject to tax control. I'm going to call in statistics at this point that are missing in the justification for the bill. On the basis of research carried out by the National Bank of Poland, it was found that in general companies in Poland carried out as many as 98% of the transactions in a non-cash manner. This is the 2013 results, so we can assume that this percentage is higher. The aim of the project was therefore achieved by market forces, without the need for legislative interference. However, the applicant also pointed to the second objective of the Act, namely to increase the control of cash flows, which could result in an increase in revenue from taxable income. Again, this is not reflected in the numerical estimates of what the scale would be. And the facts are: in recent years, the process of ailment of the Polish tax system has deepened, which has become the most visible manifestation of which is the growing difference between the revenue to the budget, which could be expected based on the state of the economy and those actually reaching it. Estimates show that in 2015 alone, such a VAT gap reached the level of PLN 53 billion. It is harder to estimate the CIT gap than in the case of VAT. However, the trend is worrying here because in 2008 the budget revenue from CIT accounted for 2.8% GDP, after 6 years, only 1.7%. Of course, a large part of this decrease could have been caused by a deterioration in the business conditions or a decrease in the profitability of economic operators, but some are simply due to aggressive tax optimization. In order to achieve the purpose of the Act to a large extent, a profound reform of the tax system must be carried out, e.g. start by changing the VAT accounting principle, implement solutions such as the central invoice base, because this would allow [[♪ The bell ♪]] reduce VAT recovery fraud, which mostly creates a gap in the budget. The direction of the proposed changes is positive.

I ask Mr Daniel Milewski to present a report to the committee. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u430	Dear Marshal, High Seym! Please accept the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the adoption of the Senate on the Law amending the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters. The Marshal of the Sejm referred the Senate's resolution on this matter to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights for consideration. We have two amendments in the Senate resolution. According to the Senate, the Act in its present form narrows down the number of entities entitled to bear lower judicial costs, providing that a relative fee of 5% the value of the subject-matter of the dispute or the subject-matter of the appeal - but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 1000 - will concern claims arising out of banking activities carried out by the consumer or the natural person running the family holding. It should be noted that such a provision implies, first, that a lower fee cannot be used by entities other than those mentioned in the provision, e.g. banks, secondly, use of a lower comparative fee by the consumer (a natural person running a family holding) when bringing an appeal (a means of appeal) is only possible if they have initiated proceedings at first instance, suggesting the wording of the consumer's findings (...). While there is no doubt as to the adequacy of this provision in terms of obtaining the first of these effects - the elimination of operators with greater economic opportunities - it is not clear whether it was the intention of the legislator to distinguish between the amount of court fees incurred in relation to the consumers themselves (hosting a family holding). In view of the above, the Senate, when adopting Amendment 1, decided that the regulation contained in Article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a The law on judicial costs in civil matters is intended to apply to the consumer (host of a family holding) whether or not he is the entity initiating judicial proceedings. Recognizing that it is appropriate to extend the catalogue of protected entities entitled to bear lower judicial costs in the case of the recovery of claims arising from bank operations, to those who generate income from non-agricultural economic activities and pay flat-rate income tax for this purpose, the Senate has adopted Amendment 2. Amendment 1 is therefore replaced by the words "collected from the consumer or the natural person in charge'. Amendment 2. - the words "family holding" shall be inserted after the words "family holding" or "profit-making" from non-agricultural economic activities and paying a flat-rate income tax. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights, after considering this resolution of the Senate at its meeting on 15 March 2016, asks the High Sejm to accept the amendment in point 1, the amendment in point 2 to reject. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u431	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear three minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Bartłomiej Wróblewski, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u432	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Senate Resolution on the Law amending the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters. We support the main aim of the project to increase the protection of consumers and producers of family farms, which are charged court fees in matters arising from banking activities as referred to in Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 2 of the Act of 29 August 1997. Bank law. In a dispute between a consumer or an agricultural producer holding a family holding and a bank, there is usually a significant economic imbalance in the potential of the parties. Therefore, the position of the consumer and the agricultural producer is clearly weaker. The obligation to pay a relatively high court fee and the uncertainty as to whether or not to obtain an exemption from that charge is a circumstance which may discourage those entities from pursuing such cases. Therefore, in our view, these entities should be given powers that facilitate the conduct of legal proceedings arising from banking activities. However, there is no reason for banks and other entities to benefit from specific regulation. The entry into force of the proposed amendment will result in court fees being charged at the general rates. In line with the ratio of the amendment, we consider the Senate's 1st amendment, positively recommended by the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, that the regulation contained in Article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a The law on judicial costs in civil matters is to be applied to the consumer and to the household operator, regardless of whether or not they are entities initiating judicial proceedings. On the other hand, we consider it insufficiently justified, in particular at this stage of the legislative procedure, to extend the circle of entities entitled to bear lower judicial costs. Therefore, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, as recommended by the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, will vote against Amendment 2 of the Senate.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u440	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am pleased to present my position on the government draft law amending the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters and the amendments made by the Senate of the Republic of Poland to this Act. At present, the provision of the article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a The law on judicial costs in civil matters shall read: "In the proceedings for claims arising from the banking activities referred to in Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 2 of the Act of 29 August 1997. Bank law, the relative fee is 5% the value of the subject-matter of the dispute or the subject-matter of the appeal, but not less than 30 zlotys and not more than 1000 zlotys. It follows from the above that the relative charge in bank proceedings, regardless of the entity that has such a claim, is currently 5% , but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 1 000. zł. The proposed amendment aims to abolish the so-called amendment. the preference of banks, but in fact abolishes the specific preference of the consumer. After the amendment, the preferential levy is intended to apply only to consumers and agricultural producers operating a family farm. Other entities, including banks, will pay a comparative fee on a general basis, i.e. 5% , but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 100,000. zł. This appears to be a fair solution because consumers and agricultural producers operating family farms are generally weaker players in the market compared to banks. However, the proposed change has a weak but insanely important point. That is, what is the situation in which the action is brought by a non-privileged party, e.g. by a bank, against a consumer or an agricultural producer and this entity will win the case in court? The bank will have to pay the court fee on a general basis, so the maximum fee will be 100,000. zł. If the bank wins, the consumer will be obliged to reimburse the bank for the costs incurred from the amount of the court fee paid by the bank. After the amendment, the fee for consumers and agricultural producers will be 5% as above. the value of the subject-matter of the dispute or the subject-matter of the appeal, but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 1 000. zł. So at the value of the subject matter of the dispute, 250,000. PLN 1 000 will be charged. zł, this will be the maximum amount of the fee. A fee for non-consumers and agricultural producers, i.e. for banks, it will be 5% the value of the subject-matter of the dispute or the subject-matter of the appeal, but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 100,000. zł. If the value of the subject matter of the dispute is 250 thousand. zł, this court fee will then amount to 12.5 thousand. zł, which is 5%. The proposed amendment will therefore benefit the consumer or the agricultural producer only if the consumer or the agricultural producer brings the action. If the consumer or the agricultural producer is a party to the defendant's case and the process loses, he will have to bear the costs calculated on a general basis, without taking into account preferential rates. Ladies and gentlemen, this could be a dozen times more than it is today. In turn, when I read the Cosmetic amendments tabled by the Senate to this law, I am convinced that the Higher House of our Parliament requires at least a far-reaching reform. On behalf of the Poselski Club Modern I would like to inform the High House that the Modern Posel Club will vote against the Senate's proposed amendments to this evil and unconstitutional law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u466	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 23 June 2015, the Management Board of Opole Voivodeship, the owner of the company Suchy Bór, by resolution of the Sejm, liquidated the only rehabilitation and treatment centre in Opole for children - in Jarnołtówek. This facility has worked perfectly for over 50 years. On the monthly rehabilitation tournaments there were an average of 60 children, giving 1 000 per year. curates from all over Poland. The resort, commonly referred to as Aleksandrówka, is located in the Opavian Mountains, in an extremely spa-friendly climate. The specific microclimate of the Jarnołtówka corresponds to the standards adopted for the health resorts of Central Europe - the 2002 PAN study. The Aleksandrówka was built in 1890 and is entered in the register of monuments of Opole. For more than 40 years, no major renovations have been carried out in the building, no EU funding has been used and no funds from the Natura Foundation. In the attic of Alexandria there is a breeding colony of a small horseshoe. The Foundation proposed to finance 90% of the roof renovation in exchange for the maintenance of the colony. The Marshal's Office in Opole, the owner of the company, did not take advantage of this proposal. By 2014 the company had a positive balance sheet. Despite the surplus received, the funds were not invested in renovations and furnishings of the building, but were collected on the bank's premises. At the Aleksandrówka Centre there was a team of special schools, which received over a million grants from the Ministry of National Education. After the closure of the centre, 35 people lost their jobs, including 13 teachers who had been certified and appointed for an indeterminate period, who did not provide employment by the school's head office in Nysa. The workers lost their jobs, and the patients were deprived of treatment and rehabilitation in the mountain microclimate. Until May 2014, the facility had a 100% contract of medical services with the NFZ. I would like to point out that in 2010 the then president of Tomasz Ganczarek received a letter from the NFZ indicating the need to equip the facility with the necessary rehabilitation equipment, i.e. centrifuge of the lower and upper limbs and cryotherapy apparatus - the value of this equipment is about 40,000. zł - to maintain the contract at the current level. Due to deficiencies in rehabilitation equipment and inadequately prepared competition offer to NFZ, Aleksandrówka received only 18% the previous contract. The amount of the contract received became the main reason for the decision by the Management Board of Opole Voivodship to liquidate the above mentioned. outposts. Following the decision of the Management Board, Aleksandrów's employees took protest measures, publicising the situation in the media, intervening with the Ombudsman, the President, the Prime Minister, the Minister of Health, Members and Local Government. As a result of these actions and the fact that it was the electoral year - local elections, 2014 - the liquidation process was stopped. Tomasz Ganczarek ceased to be President of the company on 31 July, but kept the position of President of the company Zamek Moszna - Mr Tomasz Ganczarek managed two companies at the same time. Since 1 August 2014, the Management Board of the Opole Voivodeship has appointed Mr.Suchy Bór Stanisława Łągiewka as President of the Company. Until 31 July 2014, Mr Łągiewka served as Director of the Health Department at the Marshal's Office in Opole. I would like to add that on 1 August of the same year two posts were created in the office of Marshal Roman Kolka, who is responsible for health care in our voivodship. These positions are a social policy proxy and a coordinator at the Marshal's office. Mr Stanisław Łągiewka took the position of President at the same time as Mr Stanisław Łągiewka. The new President has not taken any corrective action [[♪ The bell ♪]] in order to rescue the Alexandrówka centre, and since September last year the Aleksandrówka building is empty, it is intended for sale for an amount of 560 thousand. zł, without the form of tender.

Dude, ZWZ-AK soldier, the last Polish guerrilla who worked in anti-communist conspiracy for 24 years and died from the ZOMO bandit bullets on 21 October 1963. Józef Franczak fought in the September campaign in 1939 at the Kresy. He was arrested by the Russians, but escaped from captivity and joined the Home Army. Since 1947, he has been commanding a patrol in the WiN squad. Zdzisława Bronski, who was ambushed in 1948. He executed sentences on safe-keeping confidants, among others. Those responsible for the deaths of the commanders of the troops Lew and Uskok. Since February 10, 1953, he has been hiding alone with over 200 colleagues. He wanted to come out after October 1956, but he was facing life imprisonment. He had Mark's son with Danuta Mazur from exile. He spent it - for money - Uncle Danuta's brother, a secret collaborator of the SB Michael. On October 21, 1963, in Majdana Kozic Górnych, he was killed during the SB and ZOMO raid. With his head cut off by security, he was buried by his family in Sands. It was only in January 2015 that investigators from the Institute of National Remembrance found the skull in the collections of the Medical University of Lublin. On March 26, 2015, a third funeral was held in Sands, which was held in the assistance of the Garrison Command Warsaw. On 17 March 2008, the President of the Republic of Poland Lech Kaczyński posthumously gave the Commander's Cross to Józef Franczak with the Star of the Order of Rebirth of Poland. Józef Franczak was awarded for outstanding merits for independence of the Republic of Poland on the 90th anniversary of his birthday. His son and Danuta Mazur - Marek Franczak, who was given the right to bear his name after his father only in 1992, had previously used his mother's maiden name. Hello and praise the heroes!
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-17-sejm-14-2.u471	Today in Podkarpacie a historic event took place, the President of Poland Andrzej Duda solemnly opened the Museum of Poles Saving Jews to them. The Ulm families in Markowa. It's the first such museum in our country. It is a commemoration and honor not only of the Ulm family, but of all Poles who, putting themselves and their families in danger, saved their Jewish neighbors. This museum is a demonstration of the truth about brave Poles who showed a huge heart, helping Jews during World War II. The Ulm family paid the highest price for this aid - life. Poland must expose such people and such attitudes to the whole world, so that the truth about the heroism of the Polish nation during World War II reaches others. The drama of this great family took place on 24 March 1944. Five German policemen and policemen arrived in front of the Ulm house that morning. They were led by the league. Eilert Dieken. First Jews were murdered, then Joseph and Victoria, who were seven months pregnant. Then Dieken decided to kill the children. 17 people lost their lives. The Ulm were buried at the cemetery in Markowa, while the Jews were buried at the cemetery in Jagielle, the burial site of 41 other victims of the Holocaust from Markowa. In about 4.5 thousand Markowa Ulms were not the only family who hid the Jews. At least 20 other Jews survived the occupation in five peasant homes. Before World War II, about 120 Jews lived in Markowa. In 1995, Victoria and Józef Ulm were honored posthumously as the Righteous Among the Nations of the World, the Israeli Yad Vashem Memorial Institute. In 2003 their beatification process began in the Diocese of Przemysl. In 2004 a monument was unveiled in Markowa, which reminds us of these heroic people. In 2010 the President of the Republic of Poland Lech Kaczyński awarded them with the Commander's Cross of the Order of Rebirth of Poland. The decision to build the museum was made by Sejmik Województwa Podkarpackie in 2008 and there is a need to show facts - to a large extent unknown - indicating the participation of Poles in the rescue of people of Jewish nationality in conditions where the hiding of Jews by Poles was rigorously prosecuted and, unlike occupied Western Europe, punished with death - we read in the resolution of the intentional museum adopted by the Sejmik Województwa Podkarpackie. On 500 m2 the Ulma house was reconstructed and an exhibition hall, lecture hall and a science studio were created. The construction was officially started on 8 October 2013. The construction was completed in the first half of 2015. The project's cost - as stated at the conference - is 8 656 437 PLN, of which 1 million was transferred to the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage and 1156 thousand PLN. zł were the own resources of the Museum - the Castle in Lańcut, whose subsidiary is the facility in Markowa. The remaining amount of PLN 7 541 437 came from the budget of the Podkarpackie Voivodeship. The plot was transferred free of charge to the municipality of Markowa. It is impossible to thank all those who joined in building this place of memory today. The thanks are due to the current Marshal of the voivodship, Mr Władysław Ortyl, the entire board of directors and the councillor of the current term of office, but also to the term of office of 2006.2010. Today, before the opening ceremony of the museum, there was also a ceremony of giving national awards to Poles saving the Jews. The award was given by the President of the Republic of Poland, Mr Andrzej Duda. I am honored to be able, on behalf of the Republic of Poland, to thank all of you - these heroes, their closest ones, those who immortalize the memory of these extraordinary acts. Thanks to who you and your loved ones turned out to be, thanks also to this, the Republic can be proud today - thanked the decorated president. And then: Today I had the honour, great for me as a man, following the footsteps of President Lech Kaczyński, to mark those who did not close the door, who opened it widely and said: 'Come, we will manage', knowing what this threat is. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today Poland paid tribute to the great family and all the brave Poles. Each of us must visit the museum, and the memory of the Ulm family must be passed on from generation to generation. On 500 m2
